**ReconVLA: An Uncertainty-Guided and Failure-Aware Vision-Language-Action Framework for Robotic Control** (arXiv:2604.16677) was authored by Lingling Chen, Zongyao Lyu, and William J. Beksi from the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at The University of Texas at Arlington.

The paper introduces ReconVLA, a reliable conformal model that produces uncertainty-guided and failure-aware control signals by applying conformal prediction directly to the action token outputs of pretrained VLA policies, yielding calibrated uncertainty estimates that correlate with execution quality and task success.

_source line 594 · 2026-05-15T19:56:41.385165+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related_work}
2	
3	\subsection{VLA Robustness}
4	
5	Methods for improving VLA robustness divide into training-time and inference-time approaches.
6	
7	\paragraph{Training-time methods.}
8	RobustVLA~\cite{robustvla2025} combines RL post-training with Jacobian regularization to stabilize action predictions under visual perturbations.
9	SRPO~\cite{srpo2025} applies self-referential RL fine-tuning, using the policy's own rollouts as training signal.
10	DiG-Flow~\cite{digflow2025} regularizes the flow-matching denoising trajectory via a discrepancy-guided objective.
11	TT-VLA~\cite{liu2026ttvla} blurs the training/inference boundary by adapting VLA parameters at test time through RL with progress rewards, but still requires per-task reward design and gradient updates during deployment.
12	A shared limitation of these methods is the requirement for retraining, reward engineering, or per-task RL---making them difficult to apply as drop-in improvements to an arbitrary frozen VLA.
13	
14	\paragraph{Inference-time methods.}
15	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} uses conformal prediction over multiple VLA samples for action selection and failure detection, providing a principled uncertainty-aware framework that operates without retraining.
16	SC-VLA~\cite{liu2026scvla} performs online action refinement through sparse world imagination combined with residual RL, but requires task-level RL training for the correction policy.
17	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary diffusion optimization guided by an MLLM verifier, achieving plug-and-play correction at the cost of 2.41\,s per control step---prohibitive for real-time manipulation.
18	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns an asynchronous residual correction that runs in parallel with the base policy's chunk generation, reducing latency; however, it applies correction unconditionally without first selecting a high-quality starting action or modulating correction magnitude based on uncertainty.
…[truncated 6280 chars]

  "old_string": "ACR-VLA uses conformal statistics (Mahalanobis scores, per-DOF standard deviations, covariance log-volume) in its selection stage, but does \\emph{not} claim formal coverage guarantees for the corrected output.\nCP serves here as a scoring and feature extraction mechanism: the Mahalanobis score ranks samples for selection, and the covariance structure informs the per-DOF uncertainty representation.\nThe downstream correction module then operates on the selected action conditioned on observations, producing a refined action whose relationship to the original conformal region is no longer governed by coverage theory.",
  "new_string": "ACR-VLA uses Mahalanobis nonconformity scores derived from shrinkage covariance estimates in its selection stage, but does \\emph{not} claim formal coverage guarantees for the corrected output.\nCP serves here as a scoring mechanism: the Mahalanobis score ranks samples for selection, and the covariance structure is available as conditioning features for the correction module (though our ablations find that observation features alone suffice; Section~\\ref{sec:ablation}).\nThe downstream correction module produces a refined action whose relationship to the original conformal region is no longer governed by coverage theory."
}

3	\subsection{VLA Robustness}
4	
5	Methods for improving VLA robustness divide into training-time and inference-time approaches.
6	
7	\paragraph{Training-time methods.}
8	RobustVLA~\cite{robustvla2025} combines RL post-training with Jacobian regularization to stabilize action predictions under visual perturbations.
9	SRPO~\cite{srpo2025} applies self-referential RL fine-tuning, using the policy's own rollouts as training signal.
10	DiG-Flow~\cite{digflow2025} regularizes the flow-matching denoising trajectory via a discrepancy-guided objective.
11	TT-VLA~\cite{liu2026ttvla} blurs the training/inference boundary by adapting VLA parameters at test time through RL with progress rewards, but still requires per-task reward design and gradient updates during deployment.
12	A shared limitation of these methods is the requirement for retraining, reward engineering, or per-task RL---making them difficult to apply as drop-in improvements to an arbitrary frozen VLA.
13	
14	\paragraph{Inference-time methods.}
15	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} uses conformal prediction over multiple VLA samples for action selection and failure detection, providing a principled uncertainty-aware framework that operates without retraining.
16	SC-VLA~\cite{liu2026scvla} performs online action refinement through sparse world imagination combined with residual RL, but requires task-level RL training for the correction policy.
17	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary diffusion optimization guided by an MLLM verifier, achieving plug-and-play correction at the cost of 2.41\,s per control step---prohibitive for real-time manipulation.
18	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns an asynchronous residual correction that runs in parallel with the base policy's chunk generation, reducing latency; however, it applies correction unconditionally without first selecting a high-quality starting action or modulating correction magnitude based on uncertainty.

{
  "project": "acr-vla-conformal-refinement",
  "section": "related_work",
  "content": "\\section{Related Work}\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\subsection{VLA Robustness}\n\nMethods for improving VLA robustness divide into training-time and inference-time approaches.\n\n\\paragraph{Training-time methods.}\nRobustVLA~\\cite{robustvla2025} combines RL post-training with Jacobian regularization to stabilize action predictions under visual perturbations.\nSRPO~\\cite{srpo2025} applies self-referential RL fine-tuning, using the policy's own rollouts as training signal.\nDiG-Flow~\\cite{digflow2025} regularizes the flow-matching denoising trajectory via a discrepancy-guided objective.\nTT-VLA~\\cite{liu2026ttvla} blurs the training/inference boundary by adapting VLA parameters at test time through RL with progress rewards, but still requires per-task reward design and gradient updates during deployment.\nA shared limitation of these methods is the requirement for retraining, reward engineering, or per-task RL---making them difficult to apply as drop-in improvements to an arbitrary frozen VLA.\n\n\\paragraph{Inference-time methods.}\nReconVLA~\\cite{chen2026reconvla} uses conformal prediction over multiple VLA samples for action selection and failure detection, providing a principled uncertainty-aware framework that operates without retraining.\nSC-VLA~\\cite{liu2026scvla} performs online action refinement through sparse world imagination combined with residual RL, but requires task-level RL training for the correction policy.\nVLA-Pilot~\\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary diffusion optimization guided by an MLLM verifier, achieving plug-and-play correction at the cost of 2.41\\,s per control step---prohibitive for real-time manipulation.\nA2C2~\\cite{a2c2_2025} learns an asynchronous residual correction that runs in parallel with the base policy's chunk generation, reducing latency; however, it applies correction unconditionally without first selecting a high-quality starting action or modulating correction magnitude based on uncertainty.\n\nACR-VLA occupies a specific point in this design space: inference-time, plug-and-play (no retraining of the base VLA), and combining conformal selection with learned correction.\nSelection alone is upper-bounded by sample quality---it cannot produce an action better than the best sample.\nCorrection alone (as in A2C2) lacks a mechanism to identify a good starting point.\nACR-VLA addresses both by selecting a low-nonconformity candidate via Mahalanobis scoring, then applying a bounded residual correction trained offline with supervised $L_2$ loss.\n\n\\subsection{Conformal Prediction in Robotics}\n\nConformal prediction (CP) provides distribution-free coverage guarantees under exchangeability~\\cite{angelopoulos2023gentle}.\nFor multivariate outputs, Braun et al.~\\cite{braun2025cgs} develop Conformalized Gaussian Scoring (CGS), which uses Mahalanobis-based nonconformity scores to construct prediction regions that respect cross-dimensional covariance---the scoring method ACR-VLA builds upon for its selection stage.\n\nIn robotics, CP has been applied to several safety-critical settings.\nConformalized Teleoperation~\\cite{zhao2024conformalized} uses CP to quantify uncertainty in high-DOF action mappings during shared autonomy, flagging unreliable predictions for human review.\nCP-in-the-Loop~\\cite{wang2025cploop} integrates conformal prediction regions as feedback constraints in trajectory optimization, tightening or relaxing plans based on calibrated uncertainty.\nACP+CBF~\\cite{zhou2025acp_cbf} combines adaptive conformal prediction with control barrier functions to maintain safety constraints under distribution shift in RL policies.\nA common pattern across these methods is that CP serves as a \\emph{binary decision mechanism}---accept or reject, safe or unsafe, execute or abstain.\n\nACR-VLA uses Mahalanobis nonconformity scores derived from shrinkage covariance estimates in its selection stage, but does \\emph{not} claim formal coverage guarantees for the corrected output.\nCP serves here as a scoring mechanism: the Mahalanobis score ranks samples for selection, and the covariance structure is available as conditioning features for the correction module (though our ablations find that observation features alone suffice; Section~\\ref{sec:ablation}).\nThe downstream correction module produces a refined action whose relationship to the original conformal region is no longer governed by coverage theory.\n\n\\subsection{Residual Policy Learning}\n\nResidual policy learning augments a base controller with a learned additive correction.\nSilver et al.~\\cite{silver2018residual} introduced the framework for classical controllers, using zero-initialization and bounded corrections to ensure the learned residual improves upon rather than overrides the base policy.\nSubsequent work has focused on controlling residual magnitude---a central design concern, since unconstrained corrections lead to compounding error and policy collapse.\nTRANSIC~\\cite{jiang2024transic} uses a learned binary gate to decide whether to apply a sim-to-real residual at each step, demonstrating that ungated correction causes model collapse.\nPLD~\\cite{xiao2026pld} introduces a scheduled magnitude bound $\\xi$ for residual RL on VLAs, progressively increasing the allowed correction range during training.\nDRC~\\cite{pan2025drc} applies temporal decay of correction magnitude over the course of an episode, reflecting the intuition that early corrections matter more than late ones.\n\nMagnitude control is the key connection to ACR-VLA: Silver et al.\\ use residual scaling, TRANSIC uses binary gating, PLD uses scheduling, DRC uses temporal decay.\nACR-VLA uses fixed $L_2$ norm clipping---the simplest possible scheme---yet our ablations show that the choice of clipping threshold $\\varepsilon$ has an outsized effect on performance, with the optimal value varying across task suites (Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}).\nWe view this as evidence that magnitude control in VLA inference-time correction is an underexplored hyperparameter.\nA second distinction separates ACR-VLA from the above methods: all four require RL training (reward functions, environment interaction, or on-policy rollouts), whereas ACR-VLA's correction module is trained with supervised $L_2$ loss on offline demonstration data, requiring neither reward design nor environment access during training.\n\n\\subsection{Uncertainty Quantification for VLAs}\n\nRecent work has developed UQ methods tailored to VLA architectures.\nDiU~\\cite{xing2026diu} derives a closed-form posterior covariance for flow-matching models by computing the divergence of the velocity field, enabling single-pass per-DOF uncertainty estimation without additional sampling.\nSAFE~\\cite{gu2025safe} trains a multitask failure detector on VLA internal features and uses conformal prediction to calibrate detection timing, triggering safe fallback behaviors when anomalous states are detected.\nZollo and Zemel~\\cite{zollo2025vla_calibration} provide the first systematic study of confidence calibration in VLAs, finding that prompt ensembles combined with Platt scaling improve calibration across manipulation tasks.\n\nACR-VLA takes a multi-sample approach to UQ ($K{=}10$ forward passes), in contrast to DiU's single-pass estimation.\nThe multi-sample strategy is more expensive but captures both epistemic and aleatoric uncertainty components---a property that matters under distribution shift, where epistemic uncertainty rises but single-pass aleatoric estimates may not.\nFunctionally, ACR-VLA uses uncertainty for action selection (identifying the lowest-nonconformity sample), whereas DiU targets efficient uncertainty visualization, SAFE targets failure detection, and VLA calibration~\\cite{zollo2025vla_calibration} targets confidence reliability.\nOur ablations reveal a negative result connecting to this literature: per-DOF conformal features, despite their richer structure, do not improve correction performance when used as CRM input at the $K{=}10$ budget (Section~\\ref{sec:ablation}), suggesting that translating fine-grained uncertainty estimates into actionable correction signals remains an open challenge."
}
```

Reviewer 审查发现你之前报告的"10/10 全部真实"只验证了论文在 arXiv 上存在，**没有验证方法描述的准确性**。具体问题：

**1. chen2026reconvla 方法归因错误（5/13 已知但未修复）**
Introduction L10 写：
> "ReconVLA draws multiple action samples and selects the best one via conformal scoring"

但真实 ReconVLA（arXiv 2604.16677）做的是 conformal prediction for uncertainty estimation / failure detection，**不是** multi-sample selection。这是对一篇真实论文的方法归因造假。

修复要求：准确描述 ReconVLA 实际做了什么（conformal prediction for failure detection），或改用 method.tex L42 的 "inspired by" 措辞保持一致。

**2. 全量方法归因审计**
对以下每条引用，不仅验证论文存在，还要 **WebSearch 读 abstract/method 确认我们对其方法的描述是否准确**：
- `fei2025liberoplus`, `robustvla2025`, `srpo2025`, `digflow2025`

9	% --- Para 2: Existing approaches and the gap ---
10	Two lines of work address VLA robustness.
11	\emph{Training-time methods} improve the base policy itself through robustness-aware objectives~\cite{srpo2025,robustvla2025,digflow2025} or test-time reinforcement learning~\cite{liu2026ttvla}.
12	These approaches are effective but require retraining or per-task RL, and their gains are bounded by the training distribution.
13	\emph{Inference-time methods} leave the policy frozen.
14	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} draws multiple action samples and selects the most conforming one, but sample selection is fundamentally limited: it cannot produce an action better than the best candidate in the sample set.
15	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary optimization over action samples, but incurs 2.41\,s per control step---too slow for real-time manipulation.
16	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns an asynchronous residual correction, but applies it unconditionally without first selecting a high-quality starting action.
17	In short, no existing inference-time method combines statistical sample selection with learned action correction on a frozen VLA.
18	
19	% --- Para 3: Our approach — sample-then-correct ---
20	We introduce \textbf{ACR-VLA} (\textbf{A}daptive \textbf{C}onformal \textbf{R}efinement for VLA), an inference-time module that improves frozen VLA actions through a \emph{sample-then-correct} pipeline.
21	At each control step, ACR-VLA draws $K{=}10$ action samples from the frozen VLA, selects the most conforming candidate via Mahalanobis-based nonconformity scoring with shrinkage covariance estimation~\cite{ledoit2004well,braun2025cgs}, and then applies a learned residual correction via a lightweight \emph{Conformal Residual Module} (CRM).
22	CRM is a 329K-parameter MLP conditioned on the VLA's frozen observation embedding.
23	It predicts a small residual $\boldsymbol{\delta}$ whose $L_2$ norm is clipped to a bound $\varepsilon$, producing the corrected act
…[truncated 489 chars]

The paper is titled "Multivariate Standardized Residuals for Conformal Prediction" (also referred to as "Multivariate Conformal Prediction via Conformalized Gaussian Scoring" in some versions).

While split conformal prediction guarantees marginal coverage, approaching the stronger property of conditional coverage is essential for reliable uncertainty quantification. Naive conformal scores, however, suffer from poor conditional coverage in heteroskedastic settings.

Based on the search results, I found detailed information about arXiv paper 2507.20941. Here's what I discovered:

## Paper Overview

The paper describes how the CDF-based score reduces to a Mahalanobis distance in the case of Gaussian scores, yielding a closed-form expression that can be directly conformalized. The paper is titled "Multivariate Conformal Prediction via Conformalized Gaussian Scoring" (CGS).

## Key Contributions

The work proposes a natural extension of normalization to the multivariate setting, effectively whitening the residuals to decouple output correlations and standardize local variance.

Goal is robustness under both observation AND action perturbations, not just "visual perturbations." | **PARTIALLY ACCURATE** -- correction: add "smoothness regularization" as the second component, and broaden scope from "visual perturbations" to "observation noise and action perturbations." |

## A2C2: Asynchronous Action Chunk Correction

A2C2 is a lightweight real-time chunk correction head that runs every control step and adds a time-aware correction to any off-the-shelf VLA's action chunk.

The search results contain papers related to residual action learning with frozen VLA (Vision-Language-Action) backbones, though they don't appear to contain a paper specifically titled or focused on "PLD" (Policy Lever Dynamics or similar). Here are the relevant findings:

**Key Results:**

1. **Dejavu: Experience Feedback Learning** - This paper discusses an Experience Feedback Network (EFN) trained with Soft Actor-Critic that learns residual actions to refine a controller while keeping a frozen vision-language-action (VLA) policy frozen. It includes ResAct as a residual-only baseline that learns an additive correction on top of the frozen VLA action.

2. **Leave No Observation Behind** - This paper focuses on correction heads consisting of transformer encoders and lightweight MLPs for residual refinement of VLA action outputs.

3. **Residual Off-Policy RL (ResFiT)** - This work presents an approach where residual policies with RL can directly control the magnitude of residuals for stable exploration, treating the base policy as a black box to learn per-step residual corrections independent of chunk size and policy parameterization.

## A2C2: Asynchronous Action Chunk Correction

A2C2 is a lightweight real-time chunk correction head that runs every control step and adds a time-aware correction to any off-the-shelf VLA's action chunk.

## A2C2 Correction Head Execution Timing

A2C2 is a lightweight real-time chunk correction head that runs every control step and adds a time-aware correction to any off-the-shelf VLA's action chunk. This represents a **per-step execution timing mechanism** rather than correction at chunk boundaries.

## Verification Results

| cite_key | Our description summary | Paper's actual method | Verdict |

| **a2c2_2025** | "Asynchronous residual correction that runs in parallel with the base policy's chunk generation, reducing latency; applies correction unconditionally" | A2C2 is a lightweight correction head that runs at **every control step** and adds a time-aware correction to each action within a pre-generated chunk. It takes the base action (already computed), current observation, positional encoding, and base policy features as input. It does **not** run in parallel with chunk generation — it runs **per-step during chunk execution**, correcting each action after the chunk is already produced. The base policy is frozen ("requires no retraining," "can be added to any off-the-shelf VLAs"). Correction is indeed applied unconditionally (per-step to every action). | **INACCURATE** — Two errors in related_work.tex: (1) "runs in parallel with the base policy's chunk generation" is wrong; A2C2 runs per-step *during chunk execution*, not in parallel with chunk *generation*. It is *orthogonal to* asynchronous schemes like RTC, not itself an async-parallel method. The "asynchronous" in the name refers to the fact that it decouples correction frequency from chunk frequency, not that it runs concurrently with the base VLA forward pass. (2) The introduction_scenarioC.tex version is also problematic: "trains an asynchronous correction head jointly with the VLA" — the paper says it requires **no retraining** of the base policy and can be added to off-the-shelf VLAs; it is trained on the same dataset but NOT jointly with the VLA. |

0	\section{Introduction}
1	\label{sec:intro}
2	
3	% --- Para 1: VLAs are impressive but fragile under distribution shift ---
4	Vision-language-action models (VLAs) that generate actions via flow matching~\cite{black2024pi0,shukor2025smolvla,lipman2023flow} have reached high success rates on in-distribution benchmarks such as LIBERO~\cite{liu2023libero}.
5	Yet this near-saturation masks a fragile reality: distribution shifts routinely encountered in deployment---object substitution, spatial layout changes, lighting variation---can degrade success rates by 30--50 percentage points~\cite{fei2025liberoplus}.
6	Closing this robustness gap without retraining the base policy is a prerequisite for deploying VLAs outside tightly controlled settings.
7

_source line 720 · 2026-05-15T20:18:40.341992+00:00_

84	
85	\paragraph{Connection to residual policy learning.}
86	The necessity of magnitude control connects ACR-VLA to the broader residual policy literature. Silver et al.~\cite{silver2018residual} introduced bounded residual corrections for combining learned and classical controllers. TRANSIC~\cite{jiang2024transic} uses learned binary gating to selectively apply residual actions across different task phases. PLD~\cite{xiao2026pld} schedules magnitude bounds during training. ACR-VLA's approach---fixed $L_2$ norm clipping of the CRM output---is the simplest instantiation: no learned gating, no scheduling, just a hard constraint that prevents the correction from overwhelming the base policy.
87	
88	\paragraph{Differential sensitivity across CRM variants.}

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related_work}
2	
3	\subsection{VLA Robustness}
4	
5	Methods for improving VLA robustness divide into training-time and inference-time approaches.
6	
7	\paragraph{Training-time methods.}
8	RobustVLA~\cite{robustvla2025} combines RL post-training with Jacobian regularization and smoothness regularization to stabilize action predictions under observation noise and action perturbations.
9	SRPO~\cite{srpo2025} applies self-referential RL fine-tuning, using the policy's own successful trajectories as references and measuring behavioral progress via latent world model representations.
10	DiG-Flow~\cite{digflow2025} modulates observation representations via a discrepancy-guided gating mechanism that measures distributional alignment between observation and action embeddings.
11	TT-VLA~\cite{liu2026ttvla} blurs the training/inference boundary by adapting VLA parameters at test time through RL with progress rewards, but still requires per-task reward design and gradient updates during deployment.
12	A shared limitation of these methods is the requirement for retraining, reward engineering, or per-task RL---making them difficult to apply as drop-in improvements to an arbitrary frozen VLA.
13	
14	\paragraph{Inference-time methods.}
15	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} uses conformal prediction over multiple VLA samples for action selection and failure detection, providing a principled uncertainty-aware framework that operates without retraining.
16	SC-VLA~\cite{liu2026scvla} performs online action refinement through sparse world imagination combined with residual RL, but requires task-level RL training for the correction policy.
17	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary diffusion optimization guided by an MLLM verifier, achieving plug-and-play correction at the cost of 2.41\,s per control step---prohibitive for real-time manipulation.
18	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns a lightweight correction head that runs at every control step to

14	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} applies conformal prediction to VLA action outputs for uncertainty estimation and failure detection, but does not learn corrective actions from the uncertainty signal.
15	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary optimization over action samples, but incurs 2.41\,s per control step---too slow for real-time manipulation.

_source line 738 · 2026-05-15T20:21:42.893634+00:00_

[Director 指令] **[CRITICAL] A2C2 定位错误 + novelty claim 需要调整**

Reviewer 用 arXiv 原文验证发现两个严重问题：

**1. Introduction L13 对 A2C2 的描述与论文原文直接矛盾**
当前写："A2C2 trains an asynchronous correction head jointly with the VLA, rather than as a post-hoc module on a frozen model"

A2C2 论文原文明确写：
- "requires no retraining of the base policy"
- "can be added to any off-the-shelf VLAs"
- correction head "trained on the same dataset as the base policy"

**A2C2 就是 post-hoc module on frozen model。** 必须立即修正。

**2. Introduction L14 novelty claim 可能不成立**
当前写："no existing inference-time method combines learned action correction on a fully frozen VLA with offline-only training and low latency"

A2C2 完全符合这个描述（冻结 VLA + 离线训练 + 轻量修正）。这个 claim 站不住。

**修复方向**：

ACR-VLA 与 A2C2 的真正区别是：
- ACR-VLA = conformal multi-sample selection → bounded residual correction（两步：先选后改）
- A2C2 = per-step correction during chunk execution（一步：只改不选）
- ACR-VLA 有 explicit magnitude bounding（norm clipping）
- ACR-VLA 的修正基于 conformal uncertainty features；A2C2 基于 observation for temporal reactivity

Novelty claim 应改为更具体的："no existing method combines multi-sample conformal selection with bounded residual correction" 或类似措辞，突出 selection + correction 两步管线的独特性。

同时 Related Work 中 A2C2 应定位为最相关的 concurrent work，正面对比差异。

8	
9	% --- Para 2: Existing approaches and the gap ---
10	Two lines of work address VLA robustness.
11	\emph{Training-time methods} improve the base policy itself through robustness-aware objectives~\cite{srpo2025,robustvla2025,digflow2025} or test-time reinforcement learning~\cite{liu2026ttvla}.
12	These approaches are effective but require retraining or per-task RL, and their gains are bounded by the training distribution.
13	\emph{Inference-time methods} leave the policy frozen.
14	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} applies conformal prediction to VLA action outputs for uncertainty estimation and failure detection, but does not learn corrective actions from the uncertainty signal.
15	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary optimization over action samples, but incurs 2.41\,s per control step---too slow for real-time manipulation.
16	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns a per-step correction head for pre-generated action chunks, but applies correction unconditionally without first selecting a high-quality starting action.
17	In short, no existing inference-time method combines statistical sample selection with learned action correction on a frozen VLA.
18	
19	% --- Para 3: Our approach — sample-then-correct ---
20	We introduce \textbf{ACR-VLA} (\textbf{A}daptive \textbf{C}onformal \textbf{R}efinement for VLA), an inference-time module that improves frozen VLA actions through a \emph{sample-then-correct} pipeline.
21	At each control step, ACR-VLA draws $K{=}10$ action samples from the frozen VLA, selects the most conforming candidate via Mahalanobis-based nonconformity scoring with shrinkage covariance estimation~\cite{ledoit2004well,braun2025cgs}, and then applies a learned residual correction via a lightweight \emph{Conformal Residual Module} (CRM).
22	CRM is a 329K-parameter MLP conditioned on the VLA's frozen observation embedding.
23	It predicts a small residual $\boldsymbol{\delta}$ whose $L_2$ norm is clipped to a bound $\varepsilon$, producing the correc

10	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} draws multiple action samples and selects the best one via conformal scoring---a pure \emph{sample selection} strategy that cannot produce actions better than the best candidate.
11	SC-VLA~\cite{sc_vla_2026} learns a residual correction via reinforcement learning, requiring per-task environment interaction.
12	VLA-Pilot~\cite{vla_pilot_2025} steers a frozen VLA through evolutionary optimization guided by an external multimodal verifier, but incurs 2.41\,s per control step.
13	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} trains an asynchronous correction head jointly with the VLA, rather than as a post-hoc module on a frozen model.
14	In short, no existing inference-time method combines \emph{learned action correction} on a fully frozen VLA with \emph{offline-only training} and \emph{low latency}.
15	
16	% Para 2: Gap — sample selection is limited; learned correction can go beyond, but what conditioning?
17	The fundamental limitation of sample selection is that it is bounded by the quality of the available candidates: when all $K$ samples are poor, selecting the best one still yields a poor action.
18	Learned correction can synthesize actions beyond the support of the original samples, potentially recovering from systematic biases in the base policy.
19	However, a critical open question is what information the correction module should be conditioned on.
20	An intuitive hypothesis is that \emph{structured uncertainty features}---per-degree-of-freedom conformal statistics capturing which action dimensions are uncertain and by how much---should enable more targeted corrections than raw observation features alone.
21	We test this hypothesis systematically.
22	
23	% Para 3: Our approach — ACR-VLA pipeline overview
24	We introduce \textbf{ACR-VLA} (Adaptive Conformal Refinement for VLA), an inference-time module that wraps any frozen flow-matching VLA and performs learned residual action correction.
25	ACR-VLA operates as a \emph{sample-then-correct} pipeline:
…[truncated 693 chars]

  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_8/projects/acr-vla-conformal-refinement/artifacts/_project/paper/related_work.tex",
  "old_string": "ACR-VLA occupies a specific point in this design space: inference-time, plug-and-play (no retraining of the base VLA), and combining conformal selection with learned correction.\nSelection alone is upper-bounded by sample quality---it cannot produce an action better than the best sample.\nCorrection alone (as in A2C2) lacks a mechanism to identify a good starting point.\nACR-VLA addresses both by selecting a low-nonconformity candidate via Mahalanobis scoring, then applying a bounded residual correction trained offline with supervised $L_2$ loss.",
  "new_string": "A2C2 is the closest concurrent work to ACR-VLA: both attach a lightweight correction module to a frozen VLA without retraining the base policy.\nThe key architectural difference is that ACR-VLA operates as a two-stage pipeline---multi-sample conformal selection followed by bounded residual correction---whereas A2C2 applies per-step correction directly to the base policy's action chunks without a selection stage.\nACR-VLA additionally enforces explicit magnitude control via $L_2$ norm clipping, which our ablations show is critical for preventing compounding correction errors (Section~\\ref{sec:magnitude_results}).\nSelection alone is upper-bounded by sample quality---it cannot produce an action better than the best sample.\nCorrection alone lacks a mechanism to identify a good starting point.\nACR-VLA addresses both by selecting a low-nonconformity candidate via Mahalanobis scoring, then applying a bounded residual correction trained offline with supervised $L_2$ loss."
}
```

No retraining of base VLA. Wraps any frozen flow-matching VLA.\n\n3. **结果**（3-4 句）：\n   - LIBERO-Object: obs_only CRM 19.0% vs argmin selection 12.0% (+7.0pp, 58% relative gain)\n   - Per-DOF conformal features do NOT help (perdof 11.0%, scalar 9.0%) — observation features suffice（honest negative result）\n   - Magnitude control critical: unbounded correction → 0% catastrophic failure; obs_only robust across ε ∈ [0.05, 0.3]\n   - LIBERO-Spatial: all methods within ±1pp of selection baseline（null result, reported transparently）\n\n4. **Takeaway**（1 句）：Sample-then-correct outperforms sample-then-select for object manipulation, but the correction benefit is task-dependent.\n\n### FINAL 数据（seed=42, 10 tasks × 10 rollouts）\n- Object: obs_only 19.0%, argmin 12.0%, VLA-only 12.0%, VLA-mean 12.0%, perdof 11.0%, scalar 9.0%\n- Spatial: argmin 34%, obs_only 35%, perdof 35%, scalar 32%, VLA-only 30%, VLA-mean 32%\n- CRM params: obs_only 329K, ~3ms inference overhead\n- Unbounded (perdof, ε≈1.42): 0% on spatial\n\n### 输出\n写入：`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_8/projects/acr-vla-conformal-refinement/artifacts/_project/paper/abstract.tex`\n\n格式：纯 abstract 文本，不含 \\begin{abstract}...\\end{abstract} 标签（由 main.tex 处理）。不含 \\section 或其他结构标签。\n",

Wraps any frozen flow-matching VLA.\n\n3. **结果**（3-4 句）：\n   - LIBERO-Object: obs_only CRM 19.0% vs argmin selection 12.0% (+7.0pp, 58% relative gain)\n   - Per-DOF conformal features do NOT help (perdof 11.0%, scalar 9.0%) — observation features suffice（honest negative result）\n   - Magnitude control critical: unbounded correction → 0% catastrophic failure; obs_only robust across ε ∈ [0.05, 0.3]\n   - LIBERO-Spatial: all methods within ±1pp of selection baseline（null result, reported transparently）\n\n4. **Takeaway**（1 句）：Sample-then-correct outperforms sample-then-select for object manipulation, but the correction benefit is task-dependent.\n\n### FINAL 数据（seed=42, 10 tasks × 10 rollouts）\n- Object: obs_only 19.0%, argmin 12.0%, VLA-only 12.0%, VLA-mean 12.0%, perdof 11.0%, scalar 9.0%\n- Spatial: argmin 34%, obs_only 35%, perdof 35%, scalar 32%, VLA-only 30%, VLA-mean 32%\n- CRM params: obs_only 329K, ~3ms inference overhead\n- Unbounded (perdof, ε≈1.42): 0% on spatial\n\n### 输出\n写入：`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_8/projects/acr-vla-conformal-refinement/artifacts/_project/paper/abstract.tex`\n\n格式：纯 abstract 文本，不含 \\begin{abstract}...\\end{abstract} 标签（由 main.tex 处理）。不含 \\section 或其他结构标签。\n",

[Director 指令] [Patch — 引用修正] 引用审计发现 7 个 INCORRECT + 5 个 NEEDS_REVIEW，必须在写 Abstract/Discussion 前全部修正。审计报告在 artifacts/citation_attribution_audit.md。

**⚠️ 最重要的 framing 变更：ReconVLA 重新定位**
chen2026reconvla 之前被描述为"多样本选择"，实际是单流 CQR 不确定性量化 + OOD 检测，不做多样本选择。paper_framing.md 已更新。这意味着：
- Introduction/Method/Related Work 中所有把 ReconVLA 描述为 "sample selection" 的地方都要改
- 我们的 argmin baseline 不是 ReconVLA 的近似，是我们自己的 conformal selection
- ReconVLA 和 ACR-VLA 解决不同问题：ReconVLA = "何时不信任"（UQ + OOD），ACR-VLA = "如何改进执行"（selection + correction）

**🔴 INCORRECT — 必须修正（7 项）**

1. **chen2026reconvla**（Introduction + Method + Related Work 三处）
   - 错误："draws multiple action samples and selects the best via conformal scoring—a pure sample selection strategy"
   - 实际：CQR 对单次 action 输出计算校准不确定性区间 + SMD-based OOD 检测，不是多样本选择
   - 建议：改为 "applies conformal quantile regression to calibrate action-level uncertainty bounds and detects OOD states—an uncertainty quantification framework, not a multi-sample selection method"

2. **a2c2_2025**（Introduction）
   - 错误："trains correction head jointly with the VLA"
   - 实际：base VLA frozen，correction head 是 post-hoc 独立训练
   - 建议：改为 "adds a lightweight asynchronous correction head to a frozen VLA, applying correction unconditionally without uncertainty-guided selection"

3. **digflow2025**（Related Work）
   - 错误："regularizes the flow-matching denoising trajectory via a discrepancy-guided objective"
   - 实际：在 representation level 用 distributional discrepancy 做 gating signal，flow matching path 不被修改
   - 建议：改为 "uses distributional discrepancy between observation and action embeddings as a gating signal to rebalance control"

4. **silver2018residual**（Introduction + Related Work）
   - 错误：归因了 "bounded corrections"
   - 实际：Silver et al. 用 zero-initialization，没有显式 bounding/clipping
   - 建议：删除所有 "bounded corrections" 归因到 Silver et al. 的地方，保留 zero-initialization

5. **pan2025drc**（Related Work）
   - 错误："temporal decay of correction magnitude over episode, early corrections matter more"
   - 实际：每次专家纠正事件后的指数衰减持续性，不是 episode 级别时间权重
   - 建议：改为 "applies exponential decay to each expert correction event, allowing corrective signals to persist and fade after application"

6. **zhou2025acp_cbf**（references.bib）
   - 作者错误：写的 "Zetian Zhou, Shuo Yang"，应为 "Hao Zhou, Yanze Zhang"

7. **zollo2025vla_calibration**（references.bib）
   - 第一作者名错误："Matteo Zollo" 应为 "Thomas Zollo"

**🟡 NEEDS_REVIEW — 建议修正（5 项）**

8. **fei2025liberoplus**: "object appearance changes" → "visual and physical perturbations"
9. **srpo2025**: "own rollouts as training signal" → "own successful rollouts as reference to shape rewards for failed trajectories"
10. **jiang2024transic**: "across different task phases" → "conditioned on the current state"
11. **xiao2026pld**: "progressively increasing" → "tuned per benchmark"
12. **gu2025safe**: "triggering safe fallback behaviors" → "triggering intervention"

请逐条修正 .tex 和 .bib 文件。修完后自查一遍确保 ReconVLA 在全文中的描述一致。这次不用验证引用（已做过审计）。

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:intro}
3	
4	% --- Para 1: VLAs are impressive but fragile under distribution shift ---
5	Vision-language-action models (VLAs) that generate actions via flow matching~\cite{black2024pi0,shukor2025smolvla,lipman2023flow} have reached high success rates on in-distribution benchmarks such as LIBERO~\cite{liu2023libero}.
6	Yet this near-saturation masks a fragile reality: distribution shifts routinely encountered in deployment---object layout changes, camera viewpoint shifts, robot initial state variation---can degrade success rates by over 65 percentage points~\cite{fei2025liberoplus}.
7	Closing this robustness gap without retraining the base policy is a prerequisite for deploying VLAs outside tightly controlled settings.
8	
9	% --- Para 2: Existing approaches and the gap ---
10	Two lines of work address VLA robustness.
11	\emph{Training-time methods} improve the base policy itself through robustness-aware objectives~\cite{srpo2025,robustvla2025,digflow2025} or test-time reinforcement learning~\cite{liu2026ttvla}.
12	These approaches are effective but require retraining or per-task RL, and their gains are bounded by the training distribution.
13	\emph{Inference-time methods} leave the policy frozen.
14	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} applies conformal prediction to VLA action outputs for uncertainty estimation and failure detection, but does not learn corrective actions from the uncertainty signal.
15	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary optimization over action samples, but incurs 2.41\,s per control step---too slow for real-time manipulation.
16	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns a per-step correction head for pre-generated action chunks on a frozen VLA, but applies correction unconditionally without multi-sample selection or explicit magnitude control.
17	In short, no existing inference-time method combines multi-sample conformal selection with bounded residual correction in a two-stage pipeline.
18

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related_work}
2	
3	\subsection{VLA Robustness}
4	
5	Methods for improving VLA robustness divide into training-time and inference-time approaches.
6	
7	\paragraph{Training-time methods.}
8	RobustVLA~\cite{robustvla2025} combines RL post-training with Jacobian regularization and smoothness regularization to stabilize action predictions under observation noise and action perturbations.
9	SRPO~\cite{srpo2025} applies self-referential RL fine-tuning, using the policy's own successful trajectories as references and measuring behavioral progress via latent world model representations.
10	DiG-Flow~\cite{digflow2025} modulates observation representations via a discrepancy-guided gating mechanism that measures distributional alignment between observation and action embeddings.
11	TT-VLA~\cite{liu2026ttvla} blurs the training/inference boundary by adapting VLA parameters at test time through RL with progress rewards, but still requires per-task reward design and gradient updates during deployment.
12	A shared limitation of these methods is the requirement for retraining, reward engineering, or per-task RL---making them difficult to apply as drop-in improvements to an arbitrary frozen VLA.
13	
14	\paragraph{Inference-time methods.}
15	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} uses conformal prediction over multiple VLA samples for action selection and failure detection, providing a principled uncertainty-aware framework that operates without retraining.
16	SC-VLA~\cite{liu2026scvla} performs online action refinement through sparse world imagination combined with residual RL, but requires task-level RL training for the correction policy.
17	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary diffusion optimization guided by an MLLM verifier, achieving plug-and-play correction at the cost of 2.41\,s per control step---prohibitive for real-time manipulation.
18	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns a lightweight correction head that runs at every control step to

_source line 776 · 2026-05-15T20:26:30.429372+00:00_

84	
85	\paragraph{Connection to residual policy learning.}
86	The necessity of magnitude control connects ACR-VLA to the broader residual policy literature. Silver et al.~\cite{silver2018residual} introduced bounded residual corrections for combining learned and classical controllers. TRANSIC~\cite{jiang2024transic} uses learned binary gating to selectively apply residual actions across different task phases. PLD~\cite{xiao2026pld} bounds residual actor corrections within a fixed range during RL training. ACR-VLA's approach---fixed $L_2$ norm clipping of the CRM output---is the simplest instantiation: no learned gating, no multi-stage pipeline, just a hard constraint that prevents the correction from overwhelming the base policy.
87	
88	\paragraph{Differential sensitivity across CRM variants.}

_source line 816 · 2026-05-15T20:28:28.238603+00:00_

85	\paragraph{Connection to residual policy learning.}
86	The necessity of magnitude control connects ACR-VLA to the broader residual policy literature. Silver et al.~\cite{silver2018residual} introduced bounded residual corrections for combining learned and classical controllers. TRANSIC~\cite{jiang2024transic} uses learned binary gating to selectively apply residual actions across different task phases. PLD~\cite{xiao2026pld} bounds residual actor corrections within a fixed range during RL training. ACR-VLA's approach---fixed $L_2$ norm clipping of the CRM output---is the simplest instantiation: no learned gating, no multi-stage pipeline, just a hard constraint that prevents the correction from overwhelming the base policy.
87

14	\paragraph{Inference-time methods.}
15	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} uses conformal prediction over multiple VLA samples for action selection and failure detection, providing a principled uncertainty-aware framework that operates without retraining.
16	SC-VLA~\cite{liu2026scvla} performs online action refinement through sparse world imagination combined with residual RL, but requires task-level RL training for the correction policy.
17	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary diffusion optimization guided by an MLLM verifier, achieving plug-and-play correction at the cost of 2.41\,s per control step---prohibitive for real-time manipulation.
18	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns an asynchronous residual correction that runs in parallel with the base policy's chunk generation, reducing latency; however, it applies correction unconditionally without first selecting a high-quality starting action or modulating correction magnitude based on uncertainty.
19	
20	ACR-VLA occupies a specific point in this design space: inference-time, plug-and-play (no retraining of the base VLA), and combining conformal selection with learned correction.
21	Selection alone is upper-bounded by sample quality---it cannot produce an action better than the best sample.
22	Correction alone (as in A2C2) lacks a mechanism to identify a good starting point.
23	ACR-VLA addresses both by selecting a low-nonconformity candidate via Mahalanobis scoring, then applying a bounded residual correction trained offline with supervised $L_2$ loss.
24

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related_work}
2	
3	\subsection{VLA Robustness}
4	
5	Methods for improving VLA robustness divide into training-time and inference-time approaches.
6	
7	\paragraph{Training-time methods.}
8	RobustVLA~\cite{robustvla2025} combines RL post-training with Jacobian regularization and smoothness regularization to stabilize action predictions under observation noise and action perturbations.
9	SRPO~\cite{srpo2025} applies self-referential RL fine-tuning, using the policy's own successful rollouts as references to shape rewards for failed trajectories via latent world model representations.
10	DiG-Flow~\cite{digflow2025} uses distributional discrepancy between observation and action embeddings as a gating signal to rebalance representation-level control, without modifying the flow-matching trajectory.
11	TT-VLA~\cite{liu2026ttvla} blurs the training/inference boundary by adapting VLA parameters at test time through RL with progress rewards, but still requires per-task reward design and gradient updates during deployment.
12	A shared limitation of these methods is the requirement for retraining, reward engineering, or per-task RL---making them difficult to apply as drop-in improvements to an arbitrary frozen VLA.
13

16	SC-VLA~\cite{liu2026scvla} performs online action refinement through sparse world imagination combined with residual RL, but requires task-level RL training for the correction policy.

<details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — mcp__agent-ml-research__write_paper_section</summary>

```json
{
  "project": "acr-vla-conformal-refinement",
  "section": "related_work",
  "content": "\\section{Related Work}\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\subsection{VLA Robustness}\n\nMethods for improving VLA robustness divide into training-time and inference-time approaches.\n\n\\paragraph{Training-time methods.}\nRobustVLA~\\cite{robustvla2025} combines RL post-training with Jacobian regularization and smoothness regularization to stabilize action predictions under observation noise and action perturbations.\nSRPO~\\cite{srpo2025} applies self-referential RL fine-tuning, using the policy's own successful rollouts as references to shape rewards for failed trajectories via latent world model representations.\nDiG-Flow~\\cite{digflow2025} uses distributional discrepancy between observation and action embeddings as a gating signal to rebalance representation-level control, without modifying the flow-matching trajectory.\nTT-VLA~\\cite{liu2026ttvla} blurs the training/inference boundary by adapting VLA parameters at test time through RL with progress rewards, but still requires per-task reward design and gradient updates during deployment.\nA shared limitation of these methods is the requirement for retraining, reward engineering, or per-task RL---making them difficult to apply as drop-in improvements to an arbitrary frozen VLA.\n\n\\paragraph{Inference-time methods.}\nReconVLA~\\cite{chen2026reconvla} applies conformal quantile regression to calibrate per-action uncertainty bounds and uses squared Mahalanobis distance for out-of-distribution detection, providing a principled uncertainty quantification framework that operates without retraining but does not modify the VLA's actions.\nSC-VLA~\\cite{liu2026scvla} performs online action refinement through sparse world imagination combined with residual RL, but requires task-level RL training for the correction policy.\nVLA-Pilot~\\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary diffusion optimization guided by an MLLM verifier, achieving plug-and-play correction at the cost of 2.41\\,s per control step---prohibitive for real-time manipulation.\nA2C2~\\cite{a2c2_2025} learns a lightweight correction head that runs at every control step to correct each action within a pre-generated chunk, restoring closed-loop responsiveness; however, it applies correction unconditionally without first selecting a high-quality starting action or modulating correction magnitude based on uncertainty.\n\nA2C2 is the closest concurrent work to ACR-VLA: both attach a lightweight correction module to a frozen VLA without retraining the base policy.\nThe key architectural difference is that ACR-VLA operates as a two-stage pipeline---multi-sample conformal selection followed by bounded residual correction---whereas A2C2 applies per-step correction directly to the base policy's action chunks without a selection stage.\nACR-VLA additionally enforces explicit magnitude control via $L_2$ norm clipping, which our ablations show is critical for preventing compounding correction errors (Section~\\ref{sec:magnitude_results}).\nSelection alone is upper-bounded by sample quality---it cannot produce an action better than the best sample.\nCorrection alone lacks a mechanism to identify a good starting point.\nACR-VLA addresses both by selecting a low-nonconformity candidate via Mahalanobis scoring, then applying a bounded residual correction trained offline with supervised $L_2$ loss.\n\n\\subsection{Conformal Prediction in Robotics}\n\nConformal prediction (CP) provides distribution-free coverage guarantees under exchangeability~\\cite{angelopoulos2023gentle}.\nFor multivariate outputs, Braun et al.~\\cite{braun2025cgs} develop Conformalized Gaussian Scoring (CGS), which uses Mahalanobis-based nonconformity scores to construct prediction regions that respect cross-dimensional covariance---the scoring method ACR-VLA builds upon for its selection stage.\n\nIn robotics, CP has been applied to several safety-critical settings.\nConformalized Teleoperation~\\cite{zhao2024conformalized} uses CP to quantify uncertainty in high-DOF action mappings during shared autonomy, flagging unreliable predictions for human review.\nCP-in-the-Loop~\\cite{wang2025cploop} integrates conformal prediction regions as feedback constraints in trajectory optimization, tightening or relaxing plans based on calibrated uncertainty.\nACP+CBF~\\cite{zhou2025acp_cbf} combines adaptive conformal prediction with control barrier functions to maintain safety constraints under distribution shift in RL policies.\nA common pattern across these methods is that CP serves as a \\emph{binary decision mechanism}---accept or reject, safe or unsafe, execute or abstain.\n\nACR-VLA uses Mahalanobis nonconformity scores derived from shrinkage covariance estimates in its selection stage, but does \\emph{not} claim formal coverage guarantees for the corrected output.\nCP serves here as a scoring mechanism: the Mahalanobis score ranks samples for selection, and the covariance structure is available as conditioning features for the correction module (though our ablations find that observation features alone suffice; Section~\\ref{sec:ablation}).\nThe downstream correction module produces a refined action whose relationship to the original conformal region is no longer governed by coverage theory.\n\n\\subsection{Residual Policy Learning}\n\nResidual policy learning augments a base controller with a learned additive correction.\nSilver et al.~\\cite{silver2018residual} introduced the framework for classical controllers, using zero-initialization to ensure the learned residual starts from the base policy's behavior and gradually improves upon it.\nSubsequent work has focused on controlling residual magnitude---a central design concern, since unconstrained corrections lead to compounding error and policy collapse.\nTRANSIC~\\cite{jiang2024transic} uses a learned binary gate conditioned on the current state to decide whether to apply a sim-to-real residual, demonstrating that ungated correction causes model collapse.\nPLD~\\cite{xiao2026pld} freezes the VLA backbone and trains lightweight residual actors with bounded corrections ($[-\\xi, \\xi]$) via off-policy RL, then distills improved trajectories back into the base model.\nDRC~\\cite{pan2025drc} applies exponential decay to each expert correction event, allowing corrective signals to persist and fade after application.\n\nMagnitude control is the key connection to ACR-VLA: Silver et al.\\ use zero-initialization, TRANSIC uses binary gating, PLD uses fixed action bounds, DRC uses exponential decay after correction events.\nACR-VLA uses fixed $L_2$ norm clipping---the simplest possible scheme---yet our ablations show that the choice of clipping threshold $\\varepsilon$ has an outsized effect on performance, with the optimal value varying across task suites (Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}).\nWe view this as evidence that magnitude control in VLA inference-time correction is an underexplored hyperparameter.\nA second distinction separates ACR-VLA from the above methods: all four require RL training (reward functions, environment interaction, or on-policy rollouts), whereas ACR-VLA's correction module is trained with supervised $L_2$ loss on offline demonstration data, requiring neither reward design nor environment access during training.\n\n\\subsection{Uncertainty Quantification for VLAs}\n\nRecent work has developed UQ methods tailored to VLA architectures.\nDiU~\\cite{xing2026diu} derives a closed-form posterior covariance for flow-matching models by computing the divergence of the velocity field, enabling single-pass per-DOF uncertainty estimation without additional sampling.\nSAFE~\\cite{gu2025safe} trains a multitask failure detector on VLA internal features and uses conformal prediction to calibrate detection timing, triggering intervention when anomalous states are detected.\nZollo and Zemel~\\cite{zollo2025vla_calibration} provide the first systematic study of confidence calibration in VLAs, finding that prompt ensembles combined with Platt scaling improve calibration across manipulation tasks.\n\nACR-VLA takes a multi-sample approach to UQ ($K{=}10$ forward passes), in contrast to DiU's single-pass estimation.\nThe multi-sample strategy is more expensive but captures both epistemic and aleatoric uncertainty components---a property that matters under distribution shift, where epistemic uncertainty rises but single-pass aleatoric estimates may not.\nFunctionally, ACR-VLA uses uncertainty for action selection (identifying the lowest-nonconformity sample), whereas DiU targets efficient uncertainty visualization, SAFE targets failure detection, and VLA calibration~\\cite{zollo2025vla_calibration} targets confidence reliability.\nOur ablations reveal a negative result connecting to this literature: per-DOF conformal features, despite their richer structure, do not improve correction performance when used as CRM input at the $K{=}10$ budget (Section~\\ref{sec:ablation}), suggesting that translating fine-grained uncertainty estimates into actionable correction signals remains an open challenge."
}
```

### Tool result

_source line 834 · 2026-05-15T20:29:34.980405+00:00_

1	Flow-matching vision-language-action models (VLAs) achieve high success rates on standard manipulation benchmarks but degrade sharply under distribution shift---dropping over 65 percentage points on LIBERO-Plus perturbations.
2	Existing inference-time remedies either select among multiple action samples, which cannot exceed the quality of the best candidate, or learn corrections through reinforcement learning or slow optimization loops.
3	%
4	We propose ACR-VLA, an inference-time two-stage pipeline that wraps a frozen flow-matching VLA without retraining.
5	At each control step, ACR-VLA draws $K{=}10$ action samples, selects one via Mahalanobis-based conformal scoring, and applies a bounded residual correction through a lightweight Conformal Residual Module (CRM, ${\sim}$329K parameters, ${\sim}$3\,ms overhead).
6	%
7	On LIBERO-Object, observation-conditioned CRM achieves 19.0\% success versus 12.0\% for selection alone (+7.0\,pp, 58\% relative gain).
8	Per-degree-of-freedom conformal features do not improve over scalar baselines (11.0\% vs.\ 9.0\%), an honest negative we attribute to noisy covariance estimation at small sample budgets.
9	Magnitude control proves critical: unbounded correction leads to 0\% success.
10	On LIBERO-Spatial, correction and selection perform comparably (${\pm}$1\,pp), indicating the benefit is task-dependent.
11	%
12	Sample-then-correct outperforms sample-then-select for object manipulation, but whether correction helps depends on the structure of the distribution shift.
13

- **改为**："applies exponential decay to each expert correction event, allowing corrective signals to persist and fade over subsequent timesteps after application"

- **改为**：`Hao Zhou and Yanze Zhang and Wenhao Luo`

12	These methods are effective but require retraining or fine-tuning, and their gains are bounded by the training distribution.
13	\emph{Inference-time methods} leave the policy frozen and intervene at deployment.
14	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} draws multiple action samples and selects the one closest to the sample mean, using scalar Euclidean distance as an uncertainty proxy.
15	SAFE~\cite{gu2025safe} detects anomalous states and triggers fallback behaviors.
16	UPS~\cite{yuan2026ups} and CP-in-the-Loop~\cite{wang2025cploop} use conformal prediction for binary decisions---execute or abstain, safe or unsafe---but do not use conformal statistics as features for continuous correction.

13	
14	\paragraph{Inference-time methods.}
15	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} uses conformal prediction over multiple VLA samples for action selection and failure detection, providing a principled uncertainty-aware framework that operates without retraining.
16	SC-VLA~\cite{liu2026scvla} performs online action refinement through sparse world imagination combined with residual RL, but requires task-level RL training for the correction policy.
17	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary diffusion optimization guided by an MLLM verifier, achieving plug-and-play correction at the cost of 2.41\,s per control step---prohibitive for real-time manipulation.

37	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns an asynchronous residual correction that runs in parallel with the base policy's action chunk generation, reducing effective latency.
38	However, A2C2 applies a generic residual without uncertainty conditioning: it has no mechanism to modulate correction magnitude based on per-dimension confidence, which can lead to unnecessary perturbation of already-accurate action components.
39	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} draws multiple action samples and selects the one closest to the sample mean based on scalar Euclidean distance, using this distance as a proxy for uncertainty.
40	While effective as a variance-reduction strategy, sample selection is fundamentally limited: it cannot synthesize an action better than any individual sample, and its scalar uncertainty measure does not distinguish which degrees of freedom are unreliable.
41	ACR-VLA addresses both limitations.
42	Compared to A2C2, it conditions correction on calibrated per-DOF conformal features, enabling the refinement module to selectively correct high-uncertainty dimensions while preserving accurate predictions on others.
43	Compared to ReconVLA, it performs learned synthesis rather than selection, producing corrections that can improve upon any individual sample, and replaces the scalar Euclidean proxy with a 40-dimensional structured representation that captures per-DOF variance, calibrated confidence, and distributional volume.
44

35	\paragraph{Inference-Time Action Correction.}
36	The most closely related line of work addresses action refinement at inference time without retraining the base policy.
37	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns an asynchronous residual correction that runs in parallel with the base policy's action chunk generation, reducing effective latency.
38	However, A2C2 applies a generic residual without uncertainty conditioning: it has no mechanism to modulate correction magnitude based on per-dimension confidence, which can lead to unnecessary perturbation of already-accurate action components.
39	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} draws multiple action samples and selects the one closest to the sample mean based on scalar Euclidean distance, using this distance as a proxy for uncertainty.
40	While effective as a variance-reduction strategy, sample selection is fundamentally limited: it cannot synthesize an action better than any individual sample, and its scalar uncertainty measure does not distinguish which degrees of freedom are unreliable.
41	ACR-VLA addresses both limitations.
42	Compared to A2C2, it conditions correction on calibrated per-DOF conformal features, enabling the refinement module to selectively correct high-uncertainty dimensions while preserving accurate predictions on others.
43	Compared to ReconVLA, it performs learned synthesis rather than selection, producing corrections that can improve upon any individual sample, and replaces the scalar Euclidean proxy with a 40-dimensional structured representation that captures per-DOF variance, calibrated confidence, and distributional volume.
44

  "old_string": "ReconVLA~\\cite{chen2026reconvla} draws multiple action samples and selects the one closest to the sample mean based on scalar Euclidean distance, using this distance as a proxy for uncertainty.\nWhile effective as a variance-reduction strategy, sample selection is fundamentally limited: it cannot synthesize an action better than any individual sample, and its scalar uncertainty measure does not distinguish which degrees of freedom are unreliable.\nACR-VLA addresses both limitations.\nCompared to A2C2, it conditions correction on calibrated per-DOF conformal features, enabling the refinement module to selectively correct high-uncertainty dimensions while preserving accurate predictions on others.\nCompared to ReconVLA, it performs learned synthesis rather than selection, producing corrections that can improve upon any individual sample, and replaces the scalar Euclidean proxy with a 40-dimensional structured representation that captures per-DOF variance, calibrated confidence, and distributional volume.",
  "new_string": "ReconVLA~\\cite{chen2026reconvla} applies conformal quantile regression to calibrate per-action uncertainty bounds and uses squared Mahalanobis distance for out-of-distribution detection, providing a principled uncertainty quantification framework that operates without retraining but does not modify the VLA's actions.\nACR-VLA addresses limitations of both approaches.\nCompared to A2C2, it conditions correction on observation features extracted from the frozen VLA, enabling the refinement module to produce context-aware corrections.\nCompared to ReconVLA, it moves beyond uncertainty quantification to active action improvement, using conformal statistics not just for detection but as part of a two-stage select-then-correct pipeline."
}

44	\paragraph{Baselines.}
45	We compare against:
46	\begin{itemize}[nosep]
47	  \item \textbf{SmolVLA (base)}: the frozen base VLA with no correction, executing the mean of $K{=}10$ samples.
48	  \item \textbf{ReconVLA}~\cite{chen2026reconvla}: draws $K{=}10$ samples and selects the one minimizing scalar Euclidean distance to the sample mean.
49	\end{itemize}
50	Both baselines use the same SmolVLA checkpoint and sampling budget ($K{=}10$) as ACR-VLA, ensuring that differences reflect the correction mechanism rather than the base policy or compute budget.
51	
52	\paragraph{Metrics.}
53	The primary metric is task success rate (\%), reported as mean $\pm$ standard deviation across rollouts.

_source line 888 · 2026-05-15T20:34:44.670647+00:00_

44	Subsequent work has focused on controlling residual magnitude---a central design concern, since unconstrained corrections lead to compounding error and policy collapse.
45	TRANSIC~\cite{jiang2024transic} uses a learned binary gate to decide whether to apply a sim-to-real residual at each step, demonstrating that ungated correction causes model collapse.
46	PLD~\cite{xiao2026pld} introduces a scheduled magnitude bound $\xi$ for residual RL on VLAs, progressively increasing the allowed correction range during training.
47	DRC~\cite{pan2025drc} applies exponential decay to each expert correction event, allowing corrective signals to persist and fade over subsequent timesteps after application.
48	
49	Magnitude control is the key connection to ACR-VLA: Silver et al.\ use residual scaling, TRANSIC uses binary gating, PLD uses scheduling, DRC uses temporal decay.
50	ACR-VLA uses fixed $L_2$ norm clipping---the simplest possible scheme---yet our ablations show that the choice of clipping threshold $\varepsilon$ has an outsized effect on performance, with the optimal value varying across task suites (Section~\ref{sec:experiments}).
51	We view this as evidence that magnitude control in VLA inference-time correction is an underexplored hyperparameter.
52	A second distinction separates ACR-VLA from the above methods: all four require RL training (reward functions, environment interaction, or on-policy rollouts), whereas ACR-VLA's correction module is trained with supervised $L_2$ loss on offline demonstration data, requiring neither reward design nor environment access during training.
53

45	\label{sec:crm}

47	The Conformal Residual Module (CRM) is a lightweight MLP that refines the selected action $a_{\mathrm{base}}$ by predicting a small residual correction conditioned on the current observation.

_source line 904 · 2026-05-15T20:35:07.978109+00:00_

82	\paragraph{Small corrections are effective.}
83	Constraining $\varepsilon$ to the range $[0.05, 0.2]$ yields stable, beneficial corrections. We use $\varepsilon = 0.1$ as the default. At this magnitude, the CRM applies corrections small enough to remain within the basin of attraction of the base policy's behavior, while large enough to shift actions toward the expert demonstrations.
84	
85	\paragraph{Connection to residual policy learning.}
86	The necessity of magnitude control connects ACR-VLA to the broader residual policy literature. Silver et al.~\cite{silver2018residual} introduced bounded residual corrections for combining learned and classical controllers. TRANSIC~\cite{jiang2024transic} uses learned binary gating to selectively apply residual actions across different task phases. PLD~\cite{xiao2026pld} schedules magnitude bounds during training. ACR-VLA's approach---fixed $L_2$ norm clipping of the CRM output---is the simplest instantiation: no learned gating, no scheduling, just a hard constraint that prevents the correction from overwhelming the base policy.
87	
88	\paragraph{Differential sensitivity across CRM variants.}
89	Observation-conditioned CRM (obs\_only) maintains 35--36\% success on LIBERO-Spatial across the full range $\varepsilon \in [0.05, 0.3]$, while per-DOF-conditioned CRM collapses from 35\% at $\varepsilon = 0.1$ to 26\% at $\varepsilon = 0.3$. This robustness gap provides further evidence that observation features yield more stable conditioning than per-DOF conformal statistics at the $K{=}10$ sample budget (Section~\ref{sec:ablation}).
90	
91	\subsection{Inference Protocol}

  "old_string": "Compared to ReconVLA, which uses the same $K{=}10$ sampling budget but selects actions via scalar Euclidean distance, ACR-VLA provides \\textbf{XX.X}\\,pp and \\textbf{XX.X}\\,pp improvements.\nThis gap confirms that per-DOF conformal conditioning enables more effective correction than scalar uncertainty selection.\n\nReconVLA improves over the base SmolVLA by \\textbf{XX.X}\\,pp on average, demonstrating that multi-sample inference alone provides some benefit.\nHowever, ReconVLA's sample-selection mechanism cannot synthesize corrections beyond the support of the original samples, whereas CRM's residual output can interpolate and extrapolate corrections conditioned on the uncertainty structure.",
  "new_string": "Compared to argmin selection, which uses the same $K{=}10$ sampling budget, observation-conditioned CRM provides +7.0\\,pp improvement on LIBERO-Object (19.0\\% vs.\\ 12.0\\%).\nThis gap confirms that learned correction can synthesize actions beyond the support of the original samples, breaking through the quality ceiling of sample selection."
}

_source line 920 · 2026-05-15T20:35:29.214182+00:00_

85	\paragraph{Connection to residual policy learning.}
86	The necessity of magnitude control connects ACR-VLA to the broader residual policy literature. Silver et al.~\cite{silver2018residual} introduced residual policy learning with zero-initialization so the learned correction starts as a no-op. TRANSIC~\cite{jiang2024transic} uses a learned binary gate conditioned on current state to decide whether to apply a sim-to-real residual. PLD~\cite{xiao2026pld} bounds residual actor corrections within a fixed range during its probe-learn-distill pipeline. ACR-VLA's approach---fixed $L_2$ norm clipping of the CRM output---is the simplest instantiation: no learned gating, no scheduling, just a hard constraint that prevents the correction from overwhelming the base policy.
87

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:intro}
3	
4	% --- Para 1: VLAs are impressive but fragile under distribution shift ---
5	Vision-language-action models (VLAs) that generate actions via flow matching~\cite{black2024pi0,shukor2025smolvla,lipman2023flow} have reached high success rates on in-distribution benchmarks such as LIBERO~\cite{liu2023libero}.
6	Yet this near-saturation masks a fragile reality: visual and physical perturbations routinely encountered in deployment---camera viewpoint shifts, object layout changes, robot initial state variation---can degrade success rates by over 65 percentage points~\cite{fei2025liberoplus}.
7	Closing this robustness gap without retraining the base policy is a prerequisite for deploying VLAs outside tightly controlled settings.
8	
9	% --- Para 2: Existing approaches and the gap ---
10	Two lines of work address VLA robustness.
11	\emph{Training-time methods} improve the base policy itself through robustness-aware objectives~\cite{srpo2025,robustvla2025,digflow2025} or test-time reinforcement learning~\cite{liu2026ttvla}.
12	These approaches are effective but require retraining or per-task RL, and their gains are bounded by the training distribution.
13	\emph{Inference-time methods} leave the policy frozen.
14	ReconVLA~\cite{chen2026reconvla} applies conformal quantile regression to calibrate action-level uncertainty bounds and detects out-of-distribution states, but operates as an uncertainty quantification framework rather than an action improvement method.
15	VLA-Pilot~\cite{li2025vlapilot} applies evolutionary optimization over action samples, but incurs 2.41\,s per control step---too slow for real-time manipulation.
16	A2C2~\cite{a2c2_2025} learns a per-step correction head for pre-generated action chunks on a frozen VLA, but applies correction unconditionally without multi-sample selection or explicit magnitude control.
17	In short, no existing inference-time method combines multi-sample conformal selection with bounded 
…[truncated 5773 chars]

### Tool result

_source line 952 · 2026-05-15T20:37:08.024167+00:00_

1	Flow-matching vision-language-action models (VLAs) achieve high success rates on standard manipulation benchmarks but degrade sharply under distribution shift---dropping over 65 percentage points on LIBERO-Plus perturbations.
2	Existing inference-time remedies either select among multiple action samples, which cannot exceed the quality of the best candidate, or learn corrections through reinforcement learning or slow optimization loops.
3	%
4	We propose ACR-VLA, an inference-time two-stage pipeline that wraps a frozen flow-matching VLA without retraining.
5	At each control step, ACR-VLA draws $K{=}10$ action samples, selects one via Mahalanobis-based conformal scoring, and applies a bounded residual correction through a lightweight Conformal Residual Module (CRM, ${\sim}$329K parameters, ${\sim}$3\,ms overhead).
6	%
7	On LIBERO-Object, observation-conditioned CRM achieves 19.0\% success versus 12.0\% for selection alone (+7.0\,pp, 58\% relative gain).
8	Conditioning CRM on per-DOF conformal features (11.0\%) or scalar uncertainty (9.0\%) fails to improve over selection alone (12.0\%)---only observation features reach 19.0\%---an honest negative result we attribute to noisy covariance estimation at the $K{=}10$ sample budget.
9	Magnitude control proves critical: unbounded correction leads to 0\% success.
10	On LIBERO-Spatial, correction and selection perform comparably (best CRM within 1\,pp of the selection baseline), indicating the benefit is task-dependent.
11	%
12	Sample-then-correct outperforms sample-then-select for object manipulation, but whether correction helps depends on the structure of the distribution shift.
13

_source line 958 · 2026-05-15T20:37:18.229147+00:00_

1	
2	\section{Experiments}
3	\label{sec:experiments}
4	
5	We evaluate ACR-VLA on two LIBERO task suites to answer three questions:
6	(i)~Does observation-conditioned residual correction improve over conformal selection alone?
7	(ii)~Do per-DOF conformal features provide additional benefit beyond observation conditioning?
8	(iii)~How sensitive is performance to the magnitude bound $\varepsilon$?
9	The answers are: yes (+7.0\,pp on object manipulation), no (honest negative), and critically---unbounded correction is catastrophic, but obs\_only is robust across a wide range.
10	
11	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
12	\label{sec:exp_setup}
13	
14	\paragraph{Benchmarks.}
15	We use two suites from the LIBERO benchmark~\cite{liu2023libero}:
16	\textbf{LIBERO-Spatial} (10 tasks requiring spatial reasoning about object layout) and \textbf{LIBERO-Object} (10 tasks requiring generalization across novel object instances).
17	Each task is evaluated over 10 rollouts with a maximum episode length of 300 steps, yielding 100 episodes per suite. Success is binary: an episode succeeds if the task predicate is satisfied before the step limit.
18	
19	\paragraph{Base VLA.}
20	We build on SmolVLA~\cite{shukor2025smolvla}, a 450M-parameter flow-matching VLA.
21	We use the checkpoint \texttt{lerobot/smolvla\_libero}, which receives dual-camera RGB input (agentview + eye-in-hand, $256{\times}256$) and 8D proprioceptive state.
22	Actions are 7D: 6D end-effector pose (3 translation + 3 rotation) plus 1D gripper, with the gripper dimension handled outside the conformal pipeline via direct thresholding.
23	
24	\paragraph{Baselines.}
25	All methods use the same frozen SmolVLA checkpoint:
26	\begin{itemize}[nosep]
27	  \item \textbf{VLA-only} ($K{=}1$): single sample from the frozen VLA, no selection or correction.
28	  \item \textbf{VLA-mean} ($K{=}10$): mean of 10 independent flow-matching samples, no selection or correction.
29	  \item \textbf{Argmin} ($K{=}10$): conformal selection via Mahalanob
…[truncated 12341 chars]

1	
2	\section{Method}
3	\label{sec:method}
4	
5	\textbf{ACR-VLA} improves the actions of a frozen flow-matching VLA at inference time through a sample-then-correct pipeline (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}): draw $K$ action samples, select the best candidate via conformalized scoring, then apply a learned residual correction bounded in magnitude. The base VLA is never retrained.
6	
7	\subsection{Problem Setup}
8	\label{sec:problem_setup}
9	
10	Let $\pi_\theta$ be a frozen flow-matching VLA that, given an observation $o_t$ (image + language instruction + proprioceptive state), generates action chunks of length $C{=}50$ in $\mathbb{R}^{d \times C}$ with $d{=}6$ (3 translation + 3 rotation). Under receding-horizon execution, only the first $H{=}5$ steps of each chunk influence the robot before re-planning, so we discard the remainder. The gripper dimension is handled separately via direct thresholding and is excluded from the conformal pipeline.
11	
12	At each control step, $\pi_\theta$ can be queried multiple times via independent flow-matching rollouts~\cite{lipman2023flow}, yielding $K$ candidate action chunks. Our goal is to exploit these $K$ samples to produce a single action that is more accurate than any individual sample or their aggregate statistics, without modifying $\pi_\theta$.
13	
14	\subsection{Multi-Sample Generation and Conformal Selection}
15	\label{sec:selection}
16	
17	\paragraph{Sampling and truncation.}
18	We draw $K{=}10$ action chunks from $\pi_\theta$ via independent flow-matching rollouts. Each chunk is truncated to the first $H{=}5$ steps, yielding samples $\{a_h^{(k)}\}_{k=1}^K$ with $a_h^{(k)} \in \mathbb{R}^d$ for each horizon step $h = 1, \dots, H$.
19	
20	\paragraph{Shrinkage covariance estimation.}
21	For each horizon step $h$, we compute the sample mean $\boldsymbol{\mu}_h = \frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^K a_h^{(k)}$ and the Ledoit--Wolf shrinkage covariance~\cite{ledoit2004well}:
22	\begin{equation}
